| 33 | 2026-09-22 | **Fixed a false-positive hard quarantine in `app/graph/verify.py`**: a regex hit (`app/guards/injection.py::scan()`) is still an unconditional hard quarantine — deterministic, independent of the model, unchanged. A model-only self-flag (`classify`/`extract`'s `injection_suspected`, with *no* regex corroboration) no longer quarantines by itself — it now routes through the same `needs_review` path as the GST/low-confidence checks in the same file, with a review question asking a human to confirm the document is safe before it proceeds. | Confirmed live 2026-09-22 on the deployed Lightsail backend: a genuine invoice (Lay Meng Engineering Technology Pte Ltd, document id 9, $396, 9% GST, arithmetic correct) was hard-quarantined with no way back — `security_event` showed `regex=[]`, `model_flag=True`. Root cause: ordinary printed warranty/exchange-policy boilerplate ("must be unused", "will not apply if... are not complied with") superficially resembled directive phrasing to the classifier. The old code (a single `or` across regex-hit and both models' self-flags) directly contradicted `verify.py`'s own module docstring, which already claimed the model's self-report is never trusted alone — the docstring was correct; the code had drifted from it. No docstring edit was needed, only the code. | `app/rules/transitions.py`'s `"quarantined": set()` (no legal next state) is deliberately left untouched — a genuine regex-corroborated quarantine staying a hard dead end is a separate, more defensible bar (two independent signals would both have to miss a real attack), not what this fix addresses. Document id 9 itself, already stuck on the live Lightsail DB from before this fix, is not touched by it — a manual DB fix, handled separately by the user, not scripted here. Also noticed in passing: `app/guards/injection.py::quarantine()` writes `document.status`/`security_event` via raw SQL rather than through `transitions.py`'s authority-separated `transition_document()` — a pre-existing inconsistency, out of scope for this fix, backlogged. New tests: `tests/test_rules_smoke.py` (model-flag-only → `needs_review`; regex hit → still hard-quarantines regardless of model flag). Full suite 23/23. | final |
| 34 | 2026-09-22 | **Reviewers can now see the source document, and uploaded photos are normalized client-side.** New `GET /api/documents/{id}/file` (session-gated, streams via `FileResponse(..., content_disposition_type="inline")`) serves the original bytes; the review queue and documents table fetch it as an authenticated blob (`<img>`/`<embed>` can't carry a bearer token) and render it inline. Separately, `web/src/lib/imageNormalize.ts` downscales any uploaded image client-side (no dimension over ~2000px, re-encoded JPEG, quality 0.85) before it reaches `opsApi.uploadDocument`, falling back to the original file on any failure. | Found live 2026-09-22: a real invoice photo produced badly wrong OCR/extraction (skewed, high-res, business-card overlap), and the reviewer had no way to notice because the review card never showed the source — a confirm/reject on fields you can't check against the source isn't a safety check, it's a rubber stamp. Root cause of the bad OCR itself is out of scope (`app/extract/ocr.py` does zero preprocessing, and stays that way here) — this fix addresses the two things actually in scope: making the source checkable, and reducing the phone-camera variance (resolution) that reaches it. | **404, not 403, for cross-tenant access on the new endpoint** — a deliberate divergence from `get_trace`'s existing 403 pattern in the same file (which does leak cross-tenant existence); explicit instruction prioritized over "match the file." **Orientation correction is NOT hand-rolled**, despite being asked for: a first version read the EXIF orientation tag manually and re-applied it on top of `createImageBitmap()`'s output — but `createImageBitmap` has applied EXIF-orientation correction by default ("from-image") in every shipping browser for years, so that double-applied the rotation and visibly mis-rotated the image. Caught via a live browser test (Chrome 153) before shipping, not by inspection — verified an EXIF-orientation-6 3024×4032 test photo correctly becomes an upright 2000×1500 JPEG end to end (network upload → stored file on disk → rendered `<img>` in the review card). **Do not re-add manual EXIF orientation handling** unless `imageOrientation: "none"` (proposed, unshipped anywhere as of 2026-09-22 — whatwg/html#8085) becomes real and is deliberately requested. The documents-table "source" link's blob URL is deliberately never revoked (no reliable "tab closed" event); bounded, not worth a document-viewer product. Screenshots: `docs/screenshots/ops-review-card-source-preview.png`, `ops-after-normalized-upload.png`. New test: `tests/test_auth.py::test_document_file_endpoint_serves_own_company_and_404s_for_other_company`. Full suite 24/24. No automated test for the client-side normalization (explicitly not required — depends on real EXIF/canvas behavior; verified live instead, per above). | final |
| 35 | 2026-09-22 | **`app/extract/ocr.py` now calls `pytesseract.image_to_string(image, config="--psm 6")`** instead of Tesseract's default page-segmentation mode (PSM 3, "fully automatic"). | Already diagnosed and tested by the user directly against a real stored invoice image before this session touched it: PSM 3 silently dropped a right-aligned numeric table column ("Sub Total"/"Add GST"/"Total Amount" labels came through, their values 363.30/32.70/396.00 did not); `--psm 6` ("assume a single uniform block of text") recovered all three with no regression on the rest of that document. | `evals/demo_corpus/files/*.pdf` (the suggested regression-check corpus) turned out to **not exercise this code path at all** — verified via `app.extract.pdf.has_extractable_text()`, all 8 are `True`, so `app/graph/ingest.py::_local_text` always routes them through `pdfplumber`, never OCR. Built two synthetic test images instead (invoice-style labels+right-aligned numbers; plain multi-line prose) and confirmed byte-identical `pytesseract` output under default PSM vs `--psm 6` on both — no regression found. Note: my own synthetic invoice reproduction did not itself trigger the original bug (both PSM modes extracted its numbers correctly) — the real bug depends on specifics of the actual stored image this session doesn't have access to; not something this task asked to re-derive, so not treated as a contradiction, just disclosed. No new automated test added (not requested for this fix; OCR needs real image input, which the existing gateway-free suite has none of). | final |
| 36 | 2026-09-22 | **`/ops` is now four tabs (Upload & Review, Documents, Gap Analysis, Obligations) instead of five sections stacked on one long scroll.** Real conditional rendering per tab (`{activeTab === 'x' && (...)}`), not CSS-hidden panels — verified live that an inactive tab's content is absent from the DOM, not just visually hidden. The review-queue count stays visible on the "Upload & Review" tab label even when a different tab is active (e.g. "Upload & Review (3)"). Defaults to Upload & Review (the primary action) on load. Data-fetching (`refresh()`/`useEffect`) is untouched — presentation-layer only, no per-tab refetching. | User feedback confirmed via grep: no navigation existed between the five `<section>`s, just one long scroll. | Switching away from "Upload & Review" and back **unmounts and remounts** `ReviewQueueCard`, discarding any in-progress (unsaved) field-correction edits a reviewer was mid-typing — an accepted consequence of the explicit "actual unmount, not CSS-hidden" requirement, not something this task asked to work around (e.g. with lifted edit state). Tab bar's active/inactive visual treatment (`bg-canvas`/`text-ink` vs `text-muted`, **no font-weight change**) directly reuses `Header.tsx`'s existing Calendar/Tags tab styling rather than inventing a new pattern — deliberately skips bolding the active tab because DECISIONS #27 already found that shifts layout elsewhere in this app. `PLATFORM.md`'s full polished-frame redesign (confidence scores hidden, plain-language labels, mobile-first) is untouched on purpose — separate, bigger, already-backlogged task. Verified live (Playwright): all 4 tabs render and switch correctly, `aria-selected` correct on every tab in every state, badge persists on the inactive Upload & Review tab, seeded review-item content is absent from the DOM while on a different tab. Screenshots: `docs/screenshots/ops-tabs-review-default.png`, `ops-tabs-documents.png`, `ops-tabs-gap-analysis.png`, `ops-tabs-obligations.png`. | final |
| 37 | 2026-09-22 | **Documents can be archived (soft-delete), and archiving is now the escape hatch for an expired review session.** `app/rules/transitions.py`: new `"archived"` status, reachable from every other status (received/proposed/needs_review/filed/rejected/quarantined), itself reachable from nowhere — no restore path. New `POST /api/documents/{id}/archive` (admin+, same 404-not-403 no-leak pattern as the file endpoint), routed through `transition_document()` (never raw SQL, unlike `app/guards/injection.py::quarantine()` — a separate, already-flagged issue, not touched here); dismisses any open `review_item` on that document too. Separately: `resolve_review`'s 410 (checkpoint gone after a server restart, `docs/HANDOFF.md`'s known `MemorySaver` limitation) was previously a dead end showing the user a raw internals string ("the backend restarted... in-memory checkpoint is gone") with Accept/Reject both just failing again the same way. `ReviewQueueCard` now catches that 410 specifically and replaces Accept/Reject with "This document can no longer be reviewed automatically." plus an Archive button and a re-upload hint. | Confirmed live on the deployed Lightsail/Vercel app: a genuine invoice (row 9's condition wasn't unique — any document uploaded before a backend restart hits this) got permanently stuck with no way out, and the raw error text was shown verbatim to the end user. Archive, not hard delete — the row, file, and full audit trail (extractions, trace, security_events) all stay intact; this product's own pitch is "don't lose evidence," so a casual click permanently erasing a document would contradict that. | **Supersedes part of DECISIONS #33**: `"quarantined": set()` was deliberately left with zero legal next states there, with an explicit note not to add an escape hatch "unless you think it through separately." This is that separate think-through, not a silent reversal — archive is soft (nothing destroyed, easily distinguished by status) so it doesn't weaken the quarantine guardrail itself, just gives a quarantined document (false positive or not) somewhere to go. Detecting "this was specifically a 410" needed the HTTP status attached to the thrown frontend error (`web/src/lib/apiClient.ts`'s `apiRequest` only preserved the message text before) — matching on the message string instead would have been fragile, and self-defeating given the point is to stop showing that exact string. No restore/trash UI, per explicit instruction. New test: `tests/test_auth.py::test_archive_endpoint_archives_own_company_document_and_404s_for_other_company` (also verifies the review_item gets dismissed). Full suite 25/25. Verified live (Playwright, real expired-checkpoint condition — a `review_item` with a `thread_id` never run through the real pipeline, not a mock): the raw internals string is absent from the page, Archive removes the item from the Needs Review queue. Screenshot: `docs/screenshots/ops-review-expired-archive.png`. | final |
| 38 | 2026-09-22 | **Documents tab: cards instead of a table, a status badge per document, a working "View" action, and an "Archive" action with a "Show archived" toggle.** `web/src/features/ops/OpsConsole.tsx`'s Documents tab was a plain `<table>` with no responsive handling. | Confirmed live on a phone: filename/doc_type were cut off with no way to see the rest, no status badge existed on this tab, and — per the task prompt — "no way to view the actual file from this tab at all." That last claim didn't match the code: a `window.open()`-based "source →" button already existed (added two DECISIONS-#34 turns earlier). Investigated rather than re-added: it calls `window.open()` *after* an `await`, and most mobile browsers only allow `window.open()` as the synchronous, direct result of a click — an async gap in between gets silently popup-blocked, with no visible error. That almost certainly is why it looked entirely absent on a phone. Fixed at the root: open a blank tab synchronously first, then navigate it once the blob resolves (the standard workaround) — verified live via Playwright's popup-detection, which confirmed the *original* code's popup timed out and the fixed version opens a real tab with a genuine `blob:` URL. | Chose cards over "keep the table, just wrap the text": a 4-column table has no room on a ~375px phone regardless of wrapping — the smaller change in code (no responsive breakpoint logic, one markup, not two) also happened to be the one that actually solves the problem, per the task's own explicit "whichever is the smaller change" framing. Status badge reuses the existing `StatusPill`/`STATUS_TONE` map (added one `archived` entry, no second color system). "Show archived" is a simple client-side toggle over the already-fetched `documents` list (no new query param, no refetch) — archived count and visibility are both derived, not fetched. | 48 | 2026-09-22 | **New deterministic cross-check in `verify.py`: an invoice's extracted `subtotal`/`gst`/`total` must actually appear somewhere in the source text, or the document is flagged with a new reason — a hallucination guard independent of both the model's confidence and its own arithmetic.** Confirmed live on the deployed Lightsail backend (document id 14, "Ittibaa Glazing Enterprise Pte Ltd"): the model extracted `subtotal=440/gst=39.6/total=479.6` at 92-93% confidence and it was filed with "no issues found" — the real printed values were `SUBTOTAL 110.00/GST 0.00/TOTAL 110.00`. `440 × 1.09 = 479.6`, so the fabricated numbers were internally self-consistent, which is exactly why the existing GST-arithmetic check and confidence floor both passed them. `_check_amounts_in_text()` (new) tries a few plausible string forms of each value (`"110.00"`, `"110.0"`, `"110"`) and checks whether *any* appear anywhere in `state["text"]` (already available in `verify.py`, already used for the injection scan) — only when **none of the three** values are found in any form does it add a new reason ("extracted amounts weren't found anywhere in the document's text — please confirm..."), routed through the same `needs_review` path every other reason already uses; nothing is auto-rejected or silently downgraded. | Real, live, confirmed finding — not a hunch. Tested grayscale/autocontrast OCR preprocessing on the actual image first; it did not reliably recover the real numbers either (a rounded-box summary layout Tesseract struggles with) — ruled out as an OCR-tuning fix before writing this deterministic check instead. | **Deliberately conservative, by design, not an oversight**: flags only when *all three* values are absent from the text, not just one — a single absent value is unremarkable (OCR noise, a differently-formatted number) and would false-positive constantly; three-for-three absent is a much rarer, stronger signal. **Scoped to subtotal/gst/total only** — dates and names were explicitly excluded: too many legitimate reformatting variations (OCR digit/letter confusion, date format differences) to text-match reliably the same way plain numbers can; extending this to other field types is a separate, harder task, not attempted here. **Known limitation, stated plainly rather than glossed over**: this only catches "the value doesn't appear in the text at all" — it cannot catch a wrong-but-present value (e.g. a genuinely printed but still-mistyped-by-the-business subtotal, or a fabricated number that happens to coincidentally match a substring elsewhere in the document) — a fundamentally harder problem this does not solve. New tests: `tests/test_rules_smoke.py::test_verify_flags_amounts_not_found_anywhere_in_source_text` (reproduces document id 14's exact shape — self-consistent arithmetic, absent from text — with synthetic values, not the real document) and `::test_verify_does_not_flag_amounts_that_do_appear_in_source_text` (non-regression: a correct extraction matching the real Lay Meng Engineering invoice's shape, 363.30/32.70/396.00, all present in text, must not be flagged). | 89 | 2026-09-24 | **Text the model writes about a document may only name things the document names — a deterministic grounding check in `classify`, plus a prompt/constant fix (round 15, extraction grounding; committed `c547c6f`, pushed, deployed to Lightsail per the peer session, 2026-09-24).** **The bug, reproduced against the real gateway:** a business profile headed "BUSINESS PROFILE SUMMARY" whose page never says "ACRA" was stored as "ACRA Business Profile for Sunbird Catering Services Pte. Ltd., UEN 202398765M". **Root cause, two parts:** (1) OUR OWN prompt primed it: `classify.py`'s system prompt described the statutory lane and the business profile in terms of ACRA, and the routing constant `COMPANY_PROFILE_DOC_TYPE` (#79) was literally "ACRA Business Profile", handed to the model as the label to use; (2) nothing compared the model's prose with the page. **Fix.** (a) `app/rules/grounding.py` (new, pure, no LLM, no DB): `ground_classification(result, source_text, company_name=, subject_only=)` returns a corrected copy plus plain-English notes. Called from `classify.py` after the model answers (both the normal and the schema-fallback path) and BEFORE anything is stored or indexed, so a rejected description never reaches `document`, `document_search` or the review card; it is not in `verify.py` because verify runs after the description is written. When it changes something a `classify_grounding` trace row says what and why. Rules: an acronym or a name-looking word (a capitalised word in mid-sentence or opening a multi-word name) in the description that appears nowhere on the page is invented; a leading invented name is trimmed if the rest is still a sentence and passes, otherwise the whole description becomes a fallback built only from the document type and a grounded vendor ("Business Profile", "Invoice from Acme"); **it does not call the model again** ("regenerate or fall back" was allowed; falling back is deterministic, free and cannot loop); if only the translated description invented something the localized text is dropped and the English shows. On the statutory lane the free-text `doc_type` loses acronyms the page does not support ("ACRA Business Profile" -> "Business Profile"). (b) The prompt no longer names ACRA: the statutory lane is "letters, notices and filings from a company registry, regulator or tax authority", there is a "ground everything in the text" paragraph, and `COMPANY_PROFILE_DOC_TYPE` is now "Business Profile" (old stored rows "ACRA Business Profile" still route: the match is on the slug `business_profile`, unchanged). **Vendor (second half of the request).** Checked, not assumed: the UI never infers a vendor (every label reads the stored `vendor_name`); the source was `classify`'s `vendor_name`, written by the model, which for a non-invoice document could be the document's SUBJECT (the only company named on a business profile). Rules now: `vendor_name` is blanked when the document is a business profile (`subject_only`: it is about a company, it has no vendor); blanked when it equals the uploading company itself on any non-invoice lane; blanked when no significant word of it is on the page (name mode: at least half of the significant words must be grounded, so "Acme Trading Pte Ltd" printed as "ACME TRADING" passes). An INVOICE keeps the company's own name as vendor (a company can sell) unless the page lacks it. The prompt says the vendor is the party that ISSUED or SOLD, the subject is not the vendor, and "never fill it with the only company name you saw". Fields with no page evidence stay blank. **Narrower than described, on purpose.** The request said reject proper nouns absent from the source. A literal reading rejects correct summaries, so the check gives the model every cheap benefit of the doubt: only Latin-script name-shaped tokens are checked; ordinary document words, months, currencies, tax codes and legal suffixes never count (`GENERIC_WORDS`); a token with a digit is never checked (amounts have `verify.py`'s check); an acronym is grounded when the page spells it out ("Accounting and Corporate Regulatory Authority" grounds "ACRA"); a name within one edit of a page word (two for 9+ letters) is grounded because the model reads OCR text and quietly corrects it; a sentence-initial ordinary word is not a name; a Title Case description is checked for ACRONYMS only (every word looks like a name there, so the check would fire on everything). **Broader than described.** (1) it also covers `doc_type`, (2) it also covers the translated description separately, (3) the vendor rule includes the uploader's own company on non-invoice documents and unevidenced vendors on ANY document, not just business profiles, and (4) the prompt and the constant had to change, because a post-check alone would have left the model writing "ACRA" every time and the fallback firing on every profile. **Evidence.** Real gateway, 8 demo corpus PDFs + 3 profile fixtures, old prompt: the check changed 4 answers, every change correct (invented authority, own-company vendor); new prompt: 0 changes. 27 real stored documents replayed: no model-written description flagged (the one flag was a human-edited description, which the pipeline never checks). Real recorded model answers are embedded as unit-test fixtures. Tests: `tests/test_grounding.py` (54 cases: the failing case, generalisation to invented names that are not ACRA, and the false-positive side: spelled-out acronym, OCR typo, case/dots, Malay/Chinese/Tamil, empty inputs), `tests/test_extraction_grounding.py` (11, real `POST /api/documents` with a generated profile PDF and the demo corpus, canned classify/extract answers: description "Business Profile for Sunbird Catering Services Pte. Ltd., UEN 202398765M", `vendor_name` NULL, no search hit for "ACRA", trace row present; plus regressions: demo invoice 05 still clean with no grounding trace, notice 03 keeps "ACRA" because its page prints it, lease 08 keeps its landlord, constitution 02's own-company vendor removed), and two live-gateway tests in `tests/test_gateway_live.py`. **Mutation-checked** (each fails the suite, restored after): stubbing the classify call (8 fail), accepting every token (22), rejecting every token (28), ignoring `subject_only` (5), dropping the own-company rule (2), dropping the spelled-out-acronym rule (6), dropping OCR-typo tolerance (1). **Limits, stated not hidden.** (a) A subject that is a DIFFERENT company printed on the page (a notice about an appointee) can be picked as vendor and still passes, since it IS on the page; only the prompt guards that. (b) The invoice extractor's required `vendor` field is not grounding-checked (a follow-up: it needs a review reason and four translations). (c) No back-fill: rows stored before this keep their text until re-uploaded or edited (e.g. the Sunbird document on the deployed box). (d) Human edits are never checked. (e) Names in non-Latin scripts are not checked. (f) A made-up name within one edit of a page word passes. | The user reported the model inventing facts not on the page (an issuing authority; a vendor taken from the subject). Cheap and deterministic, runs before anything is persisted, cannot loop or cost a second call, and is testable against real recorded answers. The prompt fix removes the cause; the check catches what the prompt cannot. | A few plain descriptions ("Invoice", "Business Profile") where the model was creative; a truly invented name close to a real page word slips through; the check only sees text, not meaning, so it cannot catch a wrong-but-printed name (limit a). After the reseed it verifies the state is exactly the seed (3 companies in the group, the 6 accounts and roles, the corpus documents, none in the siblings) and reports a duplicate upload (document hashes are unique across the whole database) as a failure. The reseed calls the model gateway for 8 documents, so descriptions can differ slightly from a previous seed. It reseeds what `seed_dev_db.py` seeds: documents land in the review queue (nothing is confirmed, so no events, obligations or checklist evidence exist until someone confirms them). **Decided (2026-09-25, peer session): no confirm-the-corpus option; the reset stays empty-but-clean so judges see the review flow first-hand.** **Purge (`scripts/purge_document.py`, named to be unmistakable from the app's soft "archive").** Hard-deletes one or more documents by id: the stored file, the row and its search row, its extractions, review items, traces and security events, the events it produced with the obligations (and their notifications and review items) and checklist rows derived from them, and it REPAIRS what only pointed at it: a checklist row it satisfied is reopened to `missing` through the new `transitions.reopen_expectation` (so status writes still live in that module; it refuses anything not satisfied) and then re-checked so another matching document can take over. It discovers foreign keys from the database itself instead of a list, so a table added later stops it; it refuses, changing nothing, on an unknown id, on an obligation that cites the document as evidence without being derived from it, and on any reference it does not handle; a batch is planned first and one bad id stops all of it; each document is one transaction. `--expect TEXT` guards a mistyped id; dry run by default. **What "hard" means:** unlink, SQL delete, `secure_delete`, a full-text index merge and a WAL checkpoint, with `--vacuum` optional; a test finds a unique marker in the database file before and not after. It is not forensic erasure: an earlier snapshot or backup keeps the old bytes. `secure_delete` and the explicit checkpoint are defensive: the tests cannot show either is necessary (the index merge is). **Verified:** hermetic tests (36 cases in `tests/test_purge_document.py` and `tests/test_reset_demo_data.py`), and real runs on a throwaway copy of the local dev database (43 companies, 118 documents, own working directory and server on another port): the dry run, then the reset TWICE back to back with the real gateway: identical fingerprints, everything outside the scope byte-identical by hash, no new orphaned files; the purge of a confirmed certificate with a real derived event, 2 obligations and 4 checklist rows removed the file and every row, refused a nonexistent id with exit 2 and changed nothing. The peer session then ran the fixed script itself and reported a clean run with only the three known local-artifact FAILs and no value printed anywhere (peer-reported, not repeated here).** **What happened (urgent, from the peer session):** running the script with the system `grep` printed the `LLM_GATEWAY_API_KEY` line of the git-ignored `.env` into a session transcript. **Root cause, read from the script and reproduced with a fake canary value in an ignored scratch file (the real line was masked in what was displayed):** (1) `check()` ran `grep -rIEn <hand-written excludes> .`, a walk of the working tree, and `.env` was never in the excludes, so it was only avoided in earlier runs because the Claude Code shell's `grep` is a wrapper that skips git-ignored files; the earlier "ok" lines for the secret and Vercel checks were that wrapper, not the script (this corrects my own earlier wording in #94's follow-up notes); (2) it printed the matched line's text, and it also wrote every match to `/tmp/prepush-hits.txt`, which is world-readable, so the secret sat on disk (that file, left by earlier runs, was deleted, unread); (3) a latent one: `grep ... && [ -s file ]` treated a grep ERROR (exit 2) as "no match", so a check that could not run reported ok. **Fix (three parts asked for, plus two the same root cause needed):** 1. Files come from `git ls-files -z --cached --others --exclude-standard`: what git tracks plus new files git would let you add, never an ignored file, so `.env` and `web/.env` are not opened whatever `grep` is on the PATH. (The peer asked for tracked files; including untracked-but-not-ignored ones is a superset that also catches a new file BEFORE it is added, and still excludes everything ignored.) The same directory and file skips as before are applied to that list, and tracked-but-deleted files are skipped. The real binary is used by path (`type -P grep`), so a `grep` function or alias, including one inherited through the environment, cannot change what is read. 2. Output is `file:line` only, at most five under a FAIL plus a count: matches are piped straight through `cut`, nothing is stored in a variable or a file, and the hardcoded-API-URL check, which also echoed lines, does the same (it still ignores lines mentioning localhost). 3. The two Vercel mentions are allow-listed for the Vercel check ONLY (`VERCEL_ALLOWED`: `app/main.py`, the CORS allowlist's UAT origin; `docs/PORTABILITY.md`, which documents hosting). This is narrower than adding them to the global excludes, which the peer suggested as one option: those two files are still scanned by every other check, and a test proves a secret-looking line added to `docs/PORTABILITY.md` is still caught. 4. A grep that errors is now a FAIL ("grep failed, so this check could not run"), for every check. 5. The `.env` / `web/.env` / `.vercel/` existence checks are left exactly as they were. **Evidence (a matrix of 13 checks, canary values only, all passing after a clean re-run):** in a clean environment the four content checks read ok, the Vercel mentions no longer flag, only the three existence FAILs remain and the exit is 1; the real key (and any 8-character piece of it) appears nowhere in any output, tested without printing it; an exported hostile `grep` function is not called; a secret-looking line in an IGNORED file is not read, reported or printed; the same line in an untracked file and in a staged (tracked) file FAILs naming `file:line` and never the value; a new Vercel URL FAILs by file:line; the allow-list does not exempt the secret check; a hardcoded API URL FAILs by file:line with no URL printed and the localhost line skipped; a `grep` that exits 2 makes all four content checks FAIL and never prints "All checks passed"; no matched text appears anywhere under `/tmp`; and in a clean clone (no `.env`, no `.vercel`) every check is ok and the exit is 0. Bash 3.2 (the macOS default) parses and runs it. All scratch files were removed and `git status` shows only the script. | A secret scanner that can print its own finding, or that reads whatever the working tree happens to hold, defeats its purpose. Selecting files from git makes the answer the same in every environment. | Still a grep of patterns (an unusual spelling of a secret is missed). Written before the commit; committed in `53b1982` and pushed; the backend was deployed to Lightsail first, per the peer session.** **Shape, asked for:** GENERATED lazily, on the first `GET /api/documents/{id}/thumbnail`, not at ingest (nothing in the upload pipeline changes, existing documents need no backfill, an unviewed document costs nothing). RENDERED by the call the OCR step already makes (`pdfplumber` `page.to_image`, which renders through pypdfium2; the peer's brief named pypdfium2 directly, the code goes through pdfplumber and no new dependency was added), first page only, 72 dpi, scaled to fit 320px, JPEG quality 80. CACHED on disk in `app/thumbnails.py`'s `THUMBS_PATH` (`JAGA_THUMBS_PATH`, default `./data/thumbnails`, beside `./data/docs`), one file per document CONTENT named `<sha256>.jpg` (stored files are already named by that hash and it is unique in the database, so a thumbnail is never stale and never belongs to two documents); written to a temp file and renamed into place, so a reader never sees half a file and racing renders are harmless. SERVED by a new endpoint through `FileResponse` as `image/jpeg` with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600` (the browser may reuse it for an hour, a shared cache may not keep it). Measured on the repo's 11 real PDFs: first render 7 to 129 ms (median 8), 2.6 to 5.3 KB each, a cached lookup 0.05 ms. Frontend: `DocumentThumbnail` asks for the thumbnail for a PDF (the file itself for an image, as before, nothing for other types), crops a portrait page from its top, and keeps the generic icon while it loads and whenever there is none. **Who may see one is the file's own rule:** same query, same `status != 'archived'`, same `_hidden_or_missing`, so a personal file's thumbnail is its uploader's alone and a hidden document is a 404 that rendered nothing (tested); only a PDF has one (a photo, a missing stored file and an unreadable PDF are each a 404, and a failure is not cached, so the next request tries again). **It is derived data of a possibly-personal file, so it goes when the file goes:** the purge (its logic now lives in `app/purge.py`, #99, with `scripts/purge_document.py` a thin command line over it) and `scripts/reset_demo_data.py` now delete a document's thumbnail with its stored file (only when no other row shares the file); a soft archive keeps it, like the file. **The finding:** while verifying, the local API process died with no traceback the first time 16 thumbnails were requested at once. Reproduced in isolation with a standalone script over the repo's real PDFs: two threads rendering at the same time abort the interpreter (exit code 134), one at a time is fine, and eight threads behind one lock finish 22 of 22 renders in 0.5 s. PDFium is not thread-safe, and this API runs sync endpoints and the ingest pipeline in a thread pool. My first design (two renders at a time) was therefore unsafe and was replaced: `app/extract/pdfium_lock.py` holds one process-wide lock around the render itself (only the render: OCR, JPEG encoding and disk stay parallel), taken by the thumbnail code AND by `app/extract/ocr.py`'s `extract_pdf_text`, which renders scanned PDFs the same way from background threads. **That second half is a fix to code that already ran in production:** before this round two scanned-PDF uploads processed at the same moment could have aborted the API the same way (not observed, and not tested against production). It is a two-line change in `ocr.py`, made because a thumbnail request overlapping an upload's OCR would otherwise be a new way to trigger it; those are the only two PDFium render call sites in `app/` (checked; `app/extract/pdf.py` uses pdfminer text extraction). **Evidence:** `tests/test_thumbnails.py` (31): rendering, the cache, failure not cached, hex-only cache names, removal, eight racing requests, renders (and OCR) never overlapping (a fake page that records overlap, so it fails cleanly), a child-process stress test with eight real PDFs from many threads (so a native abort fails one test instead of ending the run), and the endpoint for every role, personal, pending, archived, other-company, unauthenticated, photo, missing-file and unreadable cases; plus purge and reset tests that the thumbnail goes and a bystander's stays. Ten mutants of the backend all killed after two weak tests were strengthened (the temp-file rename and the photo short-circuit). `DocumentThumbnail.test.tsx` (6), and the card language test now checks a switch never re-requests. Browser, real logins: on Company Files 16 of 16 PDF cards showed their first page, the cache held exactly 16 files, a repeat visit added none (4 ms), the server stayed up through the 16-request burst that had killed it, a private PDF's thumbnail was 200 for its uploader and 404 for the owner, an admin, another user and a viewer, and no session was 401. The dev server mounts each card twice (React StrictMode), so it showed 32 requests for 16 documents; a production build was not measured. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/round20-thumbnails-375.png`, `-1280.png`, `-only-me-375.png`. | The card showed a generic icon for every PDF, which the peer wanted replaced by a picture. Lazy generation with a content-keyed cache is the smallest shape that needs no pipeline change and no backfill, and keying by hash is what makes it safe to share and to delete. | The cache grows by one small JPEG per PDF anyone ever views, with no eviction. Every PDF card asks on its own (no lazy loading), so a list of a hundred PDFs makes a hundred first-time requests queue behind one lock, tens of milliseconds each. A thumbnail of a personal file sits on the shared disk (the same application-level-hiding caveat as #85). At the card's 48px the picture is recognisable as a page but its text is not legible; a larger box is a design call. A render lock means one slow or huge PDF holds every other render for its duration. | tentative |

A word cloud on Search: new aggregation, read-only, no schema change.** `GET /api/search/terms` (`app/wordcloud.py`) returns about 40 `{term, count}`. Tenant isolation, the peer's non-negotiable: the rows are `WHERE company_id = <session's company>` and then pass the two rules every list already uses (`_can_see`, `_in_company_files`), so no company sees another's words, a personal file's text never enters (two independent guards; a test fails if both are removed), a colleague's upload still pending review is left out for anyone who cannot open it, and a business profile, an archived, quarantined or rejected document are never read; only the newest 500 documents are read. A viewer can call it, like search. How words are chosen (`app/wordcloud.py` explains it): letter runs only (no numbers, amounts, dates, "S$"), lower-cased, 4 to 25 letters (three-letter tokens are mostly OCR fragments, so only `act`, `cpf`, `vat` are kept), not a standard English stopword and not paperwork filler ("pte", "ltd", "invoice", "receipt", "document", "page", "total", months and days, address words); counted by DOCUMENTS containing the word, not by occurrences, so one invoice saying "toner" forty times does not outweigh five that say "insurance"; and, from 10 documents on, a word must be in at least two documents and no more than 60% of them (a word on nearly every document is boilerplate for THIS company, its own name included). **Tuned on real text, not assumed:** the first pass over the demo company's extracted text put "inv" (from invoice numbers) first and let through "hand" ("given under my hand"), "road", "terms", "effect" and one-off OCR strings; over the repo's eight demo PDFs plus five more it was mostly meaning (company and supplier names, "incorporation", "shares", "letterhead", "stationery"). Those went into the filler list; two OCR fragments ("ple", "toa") that survived because a badly read photo was uploaded twice went out with the three-letter rule. The final cloud on the demo company is 24 words (bright, harbour, office, notice, engineering, services, ...), the rest of it test artefacts of this development database. A tap on a word puts it in the box and runs that search: cheap, because Search already runs a query from a string, so it is included. Limits, on purpose: English only (a Chinese, Malay or Tamil document contributes only its Latin-letter words), no stemming, no phrases, recomputed on each request (fine at this scale), and OCR noise that repeats across two documents still passes. A backend without the endpoint answers 404 and the page then looks as it did. **B. (a) Only THREE call sites exist, all in graph nodes: `app/graph/classify.py`, `extract.py` and `derive_events.py` are the only modules that import `app.llm`, and only `PIPELINE.invoke` reaches them, from two places in `app/main.py`: `_process_upload` (a fresh upload) and `resolve_review` (resuming a paused run after a human answers). The scheduler and the Telegram bot import nothing from `app.llm`; there is no startup hook, no scheduled job and no retry loop of ours. (b) Calls per document: classify 1 and extract 1 (only the invoice and statutory lanes have an extractor: `TOOLS_BY_LANE`), and derive_events 1 (statutory lane, company documents only). A statutory document therefore costs 3, an invoice 2, a contract or other "important" document 1, and a picture, a file with no readable text, a duplicate (stopped at ingest by its sha256) and, since round 21, a personal file cost 0. (c) **The one path outside a fresh upload's classify/extract run: `derive_events` runs when a person CONFIRMS a statutory company document (`POST /api/review/{id}/resolve`, the graph resumes at `human_review` and then calls it).** Once per document: a reject ends the run with no call, a second resolve of the same item is a 404 "already resolved", and after a backend restart the paused run is gone (a 410, "re-upload"), which never re-runs classify or extract. (d) **A hidden multiplier, found and not changed:** `app/llm.py::_client()` builds `OpenAI(base_url=..., api_key=...)` without `max_retries`, and the installed SDK (openai 1.59.6, checked) defaults to `max_retries = 2`, so a timeout, a 429 or a 5xx can make the SDK send the same request up to THREE times with no loop in our code. A one-line `max_retries=0` in `_client()` would end that; it is a code change and the peer asked for docs only, so it is recorded in KANBAN, not applied. (e) Nothing reprocesses a document: `edit_document` only writes fields (no re-classify; a description in another language is a plain edit, there is no translation call); viewing, search (`/api/search`, FTS5), the word cloud (`/api/search/terms`, plain Python over stored text), the trace, the review queue, thumbnails (pypdfium2), OCR (Tesseract) and the picture caption (the local BLIP service on 127.0.0.1:8100, not the gateway) never touch the gateway. (f) **Extraction results are cached in the database and every read path reads only from it:** the `extraction` rows, `review_item.proposed_json`, `document.lane / doc_type / bucket / vendor_name / description / extracted_text`, and `trace` (with token counts and cost). `GET /api/documents`, `/api/search`, `/api/search/terms`, `/api/trace` and `/api/review` query those tables and nothing else. (g) **Where the budget actually goes, and which of it was mine:** (i) bulk seeding paths, none run automatically and none to be run now: `scripts/seed_dev_db.py`, `scripts/reset_demo_data.py --apply` (it reseeds through real uploads) and `evals/demo_corpus/run.py`; (ii) **`tests/test_gateway_live.py`: 18 live tests, about one to three real calls each, run in EVERY full `pytest tests/` whenever `.env` holds the key (`app.main` calls `load_dotenv()` at import, so the skip condition never triggers on a developer machine).** This session ran the full suite five times (643, then after the personal-upload change, 735, 748 and 749 passed), so on the order of a hundred gateway calls were spent by those runs (an estimate from the test count, not measured). The mutation-check runs used specific test files and none included the live file; `python evals/run.py` is deterministic (its golden path is skipped). **Standing rule for the next session: run `pytest tests/ --ignore=tests/test_gateway_live.py` while the gateway budget is short; run the live file only when a change touches classify, extract or derive_events.** Recommended and not done (code, not docs): make the live file opt-in (`RUN_LIVE_GATEWAY_TESTS=1`) so a plain `pytest tests/` cannot spend the budget again. (h) Round 21 lowered spend: an Only me upload makes no calls (#101). No code path was changed by this audit. **3. UNCOMMITTED when written; the user reviews and a deploy is a separate ask. It is NOT frontend only: item 7 changes the BACKEND, which takes effect on production only after a Lightsail redeploy, and that needs the user's explicit ask.** **1. Landing: the closing section is removed** (the closing line, its second Get Started button and the `home.closing` strings in four languages); the page ends with the three cards, straight into the footer. **2. Footer on a phone, and the height bug.** *Layout:* centred in one column, in this order: the mark, the tagline, the copyright, Get Started (signed out) on its own row, then the three links one per row, each a 44px tap target. From `sm` up it is exactly what it was (brand block left, Get Started right, links in a row). *The bug:* `<main>` had `min-h-[calc(100svh-65px)]`, which forced every page to almost a screenful of height, so a short loaded page had a huge gap above its footer. That rule existed so the footer would not appear under half a page and jump down as data arrived. *What I built instead, so both hold:* the minimum height is gone, and the footer is laid out at all times but kept INVISIBLE (`visibility: hidden` with opacity 0, so it keeps its space and leaves the accessibility tree) until the page has SETTLED, then fades in over 300ms (none with reduced motion). Settled means: no API call in flight for 300ms (`lib/pendingRequests.ts` counts every JSON call, because they all pass through `apiRequest`; `hooks/usePageSettled.ts` waits for quiet), reset for each new page, forced true after 5 seconds so a call that never returns cannot hide the footer for good, and NOT reset by a request made later on the same page (a save, a search), so it never flickers away. Blob fetches (thumbnails, the viewer) are not counted: they fill boxes that already have their size. Why not the alternatives: shrinking a minimum height after load would make the footer glide up visibly on a short page; a fixed delay is wrong on both a fast and a slow network; a per-page loading flag would touch every page. *Measured (real Chromium):* the signed-in home's `<main>` is 505px tall at 375 (it was forced to 747px) and the footer sits 32px under the last card (48px at 1440); with a 1.5s API the footer is first visible at about 2.1s, never before the data, and once visible it never moves (39 samples at 375, 37 at 1440, none off its final position); a page with no API call gets its footer after about 0.4s. *Cost:* the footer appears about 0.3s after a page has loaded instead of being there at once, and after up to 5s on a page whose call hangs. The phone-bar clearance still holds: the last footer line is 27px above the raised Upload button and 45px above the bar (it was 29 and 47 in #106, the taller link rows account for the 2px). **3. Home, phone: Only me is two thirds of Upload's height.** The column is a grid with rows `3fr 2fr`; in a grid of unfixed height the `fr` rows are sized from the content, so Upload's own height becomes 3 parts and Only me is given exactly 2, whatever Upload's height is. Measured ratio 0.667 in all 20 combinations (owner, admin, user, user1; English at 375 and 320, Malay, Tamil and Chinese at 375; with the review pill and without it; heights 154px against 231px in Chinese to 200px against 300px in Tamil). The gray `bg-ink/5` is kept, and the icon, title and padding are a little larger so the taller card is not an empty box. From `lg` nothing changed (side by side, equal height). Chosen over an explicit min-height, which would need a measurement of a sibling and would drift with language, and over a stacked card, which would have been taller than two thirds. **4. Document card actions.** View, Edit, Delete and Purge stay TEXT, but each is now a 44px-tall target with 8px padding either side and never wraps; the overflow menu button went from 24px to 44px. View, Edit and Delete are one group; Purge is a separate group at the far end with the overflow menu, at least 24px of margin away (their own spacing is about 20px, and the measured gap to Purge is 44px in English at 375). Where it does not fit, the far group drops to its own line under the first rather than squeezing a label. Checked in English, Malay, Tamil and Chinese at 375 and 320px: every target 44px, one line each, no overlaps, nothing outside the card; in Malay, where "Hapus kekal" is the longest label, the far group is on its own line at both widths. **5. The document viewer: the premise does NOT hold in the app, and I built nothing.** Checked live: real Google Chrome emulating a Pixel 7 (touch, mobile user agent, 412px), tap View on a PDF: the modal opens and the PDF `<embed>` is in it 200ms later, 587px tall, with no placeholder and no Open button of the app's own (the modal's only control is Close). The same is true of the only other PDF surface, the review card's `<embed>`. **What I could NOT reproduce, and it is probably what was seen:** this Chrome is a desktop build with its built-in PDF viewer, which renders inline. Android Chrome and iOS Safari do not have an inline PDF viewer for `<embed>`, and Android Chrome draws its own dark placeholder with an "Open" button in its place. That is the browser's behaviour, not code in this app, and I have no Android or iOS device or engine here to confirm it. If inline-on-first-tap is wanted on those phones, the app would have to render the PDF itself (the existing first-page JPEG endpoint `GET /api/documents/{id}/thumbnail` at a larger size, which shows page one only, or pdf.js), which is a real build and a decision, not done. **6. Purge is folded into Delete's confirmation, and it supersedes the separate Purge group of #108.** One destructive action on the card, Delete. Its confirmation is ONE dialog; for the owner only (the existing `canRequestPurge` rule, never on a personal file, never on a document already waiting to be purged) it has a checkbox, "Also remove it permanently" (zh 同时彻底清除, ms Juga hapus kekal, ta நிரந்தரமாகவும் நீக்கு), unticked every time it opens. Unticked, confirming is the ordinary delete (`onArchive`). Ticked, the message switches to what a purge request does ("Everyone else stops seeing it now. You keep seeing it, marked Purge requested, until the team removes it permanently. You can't undo this.") and the confirm button becomes Purge, and confirming calls the existing purge request (`onRequestPurge`) INSTEAD of archiving, never both. An admin sees the ordinary dialog, unchanged. The Purge button, its own dialog and the extra group are gone; the far group of the row is just the overflow menu. `ConfirmDialog` gained an optional slot for one more choice. Assumption that could change this: "remove permanently" is the EXISTING purge REQUEST (the team removes the row with the operator script, #101 and #102), not an immediate deletion; that is unchanged, and the button says Purge, not Delete for good. Verified live for both dialog states, the admin's dialog, and that cancelling then reopening starts unticked and sends nothing; the two CONFIRM paths were not clicked live (they would archive or flag local data), and are covered by unit tests that pin which call is made. **5 and 7. A rejected document (a legacy state).** One rule, `features/ops/documentStatus.ts` (`isRejectedDocument`, `documentsForCalendar`), read by both consumers so they cannot disagree: the Calendar leaves a rejected document out before it groups or counts (`DatesView`), and the card gives it a real title, "Rejected document" (zh 已拒绝的文件, ms Dokumen ditolak, ta நிராகரிக்கப்பட்ட ஆவணம்), with its file name under it. Verified with the API response intercepted so document 1 arrives as a rejected row with nothing on it: the card shows the title and file name at 375 and 1440, and on 2026-09-22 the Calendar's day list goes from 12 rows to 11 with document 1 gone. Company Files still LISTS a rejected document (only the card's title was asked); the advisor archives the one real legacy row itself. **6. File names in three places.** Confirmed as the advisor found: the Calendar's day rows already show a real label first (14px) with the file name at 12px under it, and the business-profile section already shows a fixed "Business profile" title with the file name beneath it in 14px muted grey (the code and the screenshot show this; my script's "16px vs 16px" measured a wrapper element, not the file name, so I cite the code and the picture instead); neither was changed. The real gap was the purge requests: they showed only the file name because `GET /api/purge-requests` sent no description. **A backend change, small, built:** the endpoint now also returns `description`, `doc_type` and `vendor_name`, the same columns the document cards use (still no extracted text, path or hash: the existing test that pins the key set was updated and keeps that guard), and the section shows the document's label first (`formatDocumentLabel`, so "Acme Pte Ltd · invoice" or the description in the page's language) with the file name small beneath, falling back to the file name alone when there is nothing to label it by or the backend is older (the new fields are optional in the type, which is the deploy-order safety). Checked live with the list intercepted: "Office lease for Level 3" over its file name, "Acme Pte Ltd · invoice" over `scan-0088.pdf`, and `draft-final-v2.pdf` alone, at 375 and 1440. **8. The date range inputs: sized, NOT built, and my recommendation is to leave the native picker.** Native `<input type="date">` is documented to show the date in the browser's or device's own regional format, with no prop or class to set it, and its value is always ISO underneath. **What I could and could not check:** the headless Chromium here rendered `05/03/2024` (dd/mm/yyyy) for EVERY `lang` value I tried (en-US, en-GB, de, ms, zh, ta, none) and under two different browser locales (en-US, de-DE), so this environment neither shows the regional behaviour nor shows that `lang` can change it; the claim about phones and other regions is the platforms' documented behaviour, not something I saw on a device. It also means that in the browser I test with, the inputs already read dd/mm/yyyy. What it gives: the phone's date wheel and the desktop's own picker, keyboard and screen-reader support, no library, and a person sees the format of their own device. To force dd/mm/yyyy the choice is between (a) two masked text inputs, which is cheap and the app already has one (`isoToDmy`, `dmyToIso`, the masked review-card date field, DECISIONS #67) but loses the picker and the wheel, or (b) a custom calendar popover, which is real work (a grid with focus handling and keyboard support, four-language month and weekday names, validation, tests: about a day) and also loses the native wheel. Both trade the mobile date experience and native accessibility for a display format that matters only to someone whose device is set to another region. **If a fixed format is wanted for the demo, the small option is to keep the native inputs and add a line under them that echoes the chosen range in dd/mm/yyyy ("Showing 01/01/2024 to 31/03/2024"); not built.** **9. The signed-in home fills the viewport at desktop width, built on #108's mechanism and not beside it.** `<main>` already has no minimum height (#108), so a short page's footer sits under its content everywhere. The hub opts in to the opposite, from `lg` only, through ONE list, `FIT_VIEWPORT_ROUTES = ['home']` in `router/routes.ts`: when it is signed in and on such a route, `App` wraps the header, main and footer in a column at least a screen tall, main and its page grow into it, and `SignedInHome`'s container centres the cards in that space, vertically as well as across. No page sets a height of its own. Measured: at 1440x900, 1280x720, 1920x1080 and 1024x768 the page does not scroll, the footer's bottom edge is the window's bottom edge, and the cards' centre is exactly the middle of the space between the header and the footer; a window too short to fit (1440x640) scrolls with the footer directly under the content; below `lg` (768 and 375) nothing changed; the viewer's home, the Only me page on the same route, also has its footer at the bottom with no scroll. **Bonus items.** *Currency:* nothing to unify. There is no currency formatting or `$`/`S$` anywhere in the UI code; "SGD" appears only in model-written description text ("Tax invoice ... SGD 4,360.00") and in the extracted `currency` form field, both data, not formatting. If S$ is wanted, say where (the classifier's prompt, or a display formatter on the invoice review form's total fields) before anything is "fixed". *Chips with (0):* built: a bucket with nothing in it inside the date range has no chip, except the CHOSEN one, which stays so it can be un-chosen; All is always there (locally: All (25), Receivables (1), Expenses (7), Memory Lane (9)). *Word-cloud minimum:* it already exists, but only once there are at least 10 documents (below that every word counts, or the cloud would be empty); it is now a named constant beside its siblings, `MIN_DOCUMENTS_PER_WORD = 2` in `app/wordcloud.py`, behaviour unchanged, tests pass. *Company switcher:* the cap went from 170px to 240px; measured at 1280 and 1440 in English, Malay, Tamil and Chinese with no header overlap, and the longest option, "Try Demo Logistics Pte Ltd", fits the 232px select. **Left alone as the user asked:** the three footer links stay `#` placeholders, the hero screenshot retake stays queued for after deploy, the "Backend reachable" badge stays. **Verified (tested in this session, not assumed):** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm run test` 614 passed (582 before, +32); `npm run build` clean; backend `pytest tests/` 738 passed and 18 skipped (opt-in live tests, so no model calls; +2 new for the purge-request fields); real Chromium against a local backend and dev server: review empty state 5, delete and purge dialog 9, rejected document 3, file names 4, filter chips 2, hub layout 12, switcher 12, plus the live Trace clicks; the data the local database lacks (a rejected row, purge requests, a business profile) was supplied by intercepting read responses. UNCOMMITTED when written; frontend only.** The advisor session approved either of two fixes and left the choice to me. **Chosen: latch the footer's visibility, and hold the previous page's height while a new page loads.** `App.tsx` no longer passes `usePageSettled`'s value straight to the footer: `useLatch` (`hooks/useLatch.ts`) reveals it on the FIRST page that settles and keeps it revealed, so `usePageSettled` still keeps the footer out of sight while the first page is half-built (#108) but a new page no longer hides it. `useReservedPageHeight` (`hooks/useReservedPageHeight.ts`) sets `main`'s `minHeight` to the height the old page had, read during the render in which the page key changes (the DOM still holds the old page then), and lets go once the new page settles, so there is still no standing minimum height (#108). Its release effect depends on `settled` alone: in the render that changes the key, `settled` is still the OLD page's true, and releasing then would undo the hold at once. **Measured, frame by frame, not assumed:** before, the footer went invisible on every route change and faded back in 600 to 900ms later. With the latch ALONE the blink was gone (0 hidden or faded frames) but it became a jump: for example 856, then 601, then 3429 pixels from the top going from the Calendar to Company Files, the footer hopping up under the half-loaded page and back down. With the latch and the held height: 0 hidden or faded frames in 5 navigations at 1440x900 and 5 at 375x812, and no hop under half-loaded content: Calendar to Company Files stays at 856 and then goes to its final place; Calendar to Search stays at 856 for about half a second and moves once, to 595; a taller page pushes it down as its content arrives. **Why not the advisor's other option** (a min-height on the content and a scroll reset, so the fade happens below the fold): it is the fixed near-viewport minimum height that #108 removed on purpose, because a short, loaded page then had a huge empty gap above its footer. Found because the second hostname of the AWS-served frontend (#119) could not sign in; asked for by the advisor session (user confirmed option B). UNCOMMITTED when written; frontend only; nothing deployed.** **The bug (found by read-only checks, no login):** `web/src/lib/apiClient.ts` had `API_BASE_URL = import.meta.env.VITE_API_BASE_URL || 'http://127.0.0.1:8000'`, with no same-origin option, so the box's copy had to be built with an ABSOLUTE URL, the bare-IP host. The JS served at `https://jagaos.13-251-52-222.nip.io/` had exactly that one host baked in. From the bare host that is the page's own origin, so it worked; from the branded host every call was cross-origin, and the API's CORS list (`localhost:5173`, `127.0.0.1:5173`, `https://jagaos.vercel.app`) does not include it: `GET /api/health` with that Origin came back 200 with no `access-control-allow-origin`, and a browser-style preflight (`OPTIONS /api/auth/me`) came back 400. Every JSON POST and every call carrying an `Authorization` header preflights, so the page loaded and nothing signed in. #119's own check was `curl`-only, and `curl` does not enforce CORS, which is why "verified live" looked green (the advisor session owned that gap). **The change:** `||` became `??` behind a tiny pure helper (`resolveApiBase`, with `DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL`), so an empty string stays empty and every call is the relative `/api/...`. Three cases, documented on the constant: NOT SET is `http://127.0.0.1:8000` (local development); a URL is that API, cross-origin unless it is the site's own address, with that host baked into the build; SET BUT EMPTY is the page's own origin. **A knock-on I had to fix:** `web/.env.example` shipped `VITE_API_BASE_URL=` as an empty line, which used to mean the localhost default and now means same-origin, so a developer copying it verbatim would get calls to the Vite dev server (no proxy). It now explains the three states and has the line commented out. Nothing else needed changing: the only other uses are two `${API_BASE_URL}/api/...` template strings in `opsApi.ts`, which are fine with an empty base, and there is no `new URL(...)` on it. **What did not change:** the backend and its CORS list; Vercel (its build sets a non-empty URL, which `??` keeps); local development (unset, or a URL in the ignored `web/.env`). **Verified, in a real browser, because that is where CORS lives:** (1) `apiBase.test.ts`, 6 tests, on the helper and on the wiring (empty gives the request `/api/health`, a URL gives that host, unset gives the localhost default); 704 vitest in all (698 before), typecheck and build clean. (2) Three production builds from a machine whose `web/.env.local` points at PRODUCTION: `VITE_API_BASE_URL=` (empty) gave a bundle with NO `127.0.0.1:8000` and NO production host, so a process variable beats `.env.local` even when empty and the fallback is folded away; an absolute local URL kept its one URL; the production URL was kept. (3) Chromium signing in through the demo picker as the owner on the EMPTY build served like the box's Caddy (a small stand-in: static files with the SPA fallback, `/api/*` proxied to a local backend whose CORS list allows neither origin): on `http://localhost:8080` AND on `http://127.0.0.1:8080`, two different origins from one server, it signed in, stored a session, and loaded the Calendar with data; 10 API calls each, all to the page's own origin, none to another, no console errors. (4) THE CONTROL: the absolute-URL build served the same way on `http://localhost:8081` was blocked by Chromium ("has been blocked by CORS policy: Response to preflight request doesn't pass access control check", `net::ERR_FAILED`) and did not sign in, so the harness does enforce CORS and the failure of the new hostname is reproduced. Chosen over the `sm` and `lg` breakpoints the advisor pointed to, because a width only guesses at a capability: a large Android tablet is wider than any breakpoint and has no viewer, and a narrow desktop window is narrow and has one. | 126 | 2026-09-26 | **A real gap in round 5, item 2, found LIVE ON PRODUCTION by the peer session after it had already deployed: Edit does not render at all on a purge-pending document (Delete correctly renders disabled). Root cause traced past the peer's own finding, to the actual fix, both in the same pass. UNCOMMITTED when written; backend only; the peer session holds the redeploy once this is ready.** **The trace:** `documentIsEditable(canEdit, doc)` gates whether Edit renders at all, and reads `doc.can_edit`, which `_document_row_for` (`app/main.py`) force-set to `False` for any purge-requested row since round 21 (DECISIONS #102) — dating from well before this round, so round 5's new `disabled={purgePending}` styling never got a chance to apply: the button was never in the tree to begin with. Delete has no such problem because its own permission signal, `canArchive`, was never touched by that override. **Followed it one step further than the peer had:** why was `can_edit` ever force-set false in the first place? Because it was the ONLY thing making a purge-requested document read-only — `may_see_document` and `may_edit_document` (`app/auth.py`) have never known about archived or purge-requested status at all, and `edit_document`'s own SELECT had NO `status != 'archived'` exclusion, unlike `get_document_file` and `get_trace`, which both already have one. So a direct `PATCH /api/documents/{id}` to a purge-requested document was ALWAYS accepted by the server, whoever the caller — "read-only" was a UI omission, not a rule, and simply un-hiding Edit for round 5 would have quietly turned that omission into a real, clickable capability. **The actual fix, two changes:** (1) `_document_row_for` no longer touches `can_edit` for a purge-requested row — it stays the plain ownership answer `may_edit_document` already gives, same as any other document, so the frontend's existing round-5 logic (`canEdit && !editing`, then `disabled={purgePending}`) now sees the button and disables it correctly, no frontend change needed at all. (2) `edit_document` gains `AND status != 'archived'` on its fetch, matching `get_document_file`/`get_trace` exactly, so a direct edit attempt on ANY archived document — purge-requested or not, by anyone — is now a genuine 404, not a silent success. **Verified live, both ends:** a real purge-pending document now shows Edit AND Delete disabled side by side with View and Cancel Purge active (screenshot); a `curl`-equivalent PATCH to the same document answered 404 (was a silent 200 before this fix); cancelled the test request back to `filed` after. **Tests:** `tests/test_purge_request.py`'s existing `can_edit is False` assertion corrected to `True` (an owner's plain ownership answer, unchanged by purge status) and a new test pins the real enforcement: a PATCH to a purge-requested document is refused with 404 and the database is untouched. Full suite re-run: `pytest tests/` 805 passed (was 804; +1), 18 skipped; `npm run test` 766/766 unaffected (no frontend file touched by this fix). | A UI button that merely fails to render is not a security boundary, and treating "the frontend doesn't offer it" as equivalent to "the server refuses it" is exactly the kind of gap that survives until someone tries the direct call — which round 5's own change to disabled-not-hidden nearly did, by accident, before either session caught it. Matching the existing `status != 'archived'` pattern already used by two sibling endpoints was the smallest fix that actually closes it, not a new one-off rule. | This was live on production, unenforced, before this fix (per the peer's report, both deployments already shipped item 2's frontend change) — low realistic exposure (an attacker would need a valid session and the document's own id, and the only consequence is editing display fields on a document already flagged for deletion) but a real gap nonetheless, now closed and ready for the peer session to redeploy. | final (committed and pushed as `dffff75`; per the peer session deployed to Lightsail after a file-by-file diff of every backend file against the box (only `main.py` differed) and a clean `jaga-api` restart, both frontends redeployed, and live-checked on production: Edit and Delete both rendered disabled on a purge-pending card; this session verified only the git state) |

(2) `app/extract/image_prep.py`: EXIF orientation first, longest edge 1500, never upscaled, alpha flattened on white, JPEG q80, a temp working copy deleted always; the original, its hash and its download are untouched. It runs on every image upload, and each page of a multi-page photo upload is rasterized at that cap; a scanned PDF is left at its own 200 dpi. Measured on a synthetic 3000x4000 invoice with real Tesseract, recall was 0.94 to 1.0 at full size against 0.95 to 0.99 at 1500 and OCR text length was essentially unchanged; small type (32 px) fell 0.988 to 0.951. **Unverified on real, noisy phone photos.** (3) A single-photo document's OCR text is capped at `IMAGE_TEXT_CHAR_LIMIT` = 8,000 characters (env `LLM_IMAGE_TEXT_CHAR_LIMIT`; a `TEXT_CAP` log line with numbers only when it bites); a merged multi-page upload and a PDF keep 50,000. No cache parameter is sent and none is claimed; if the gateway ever reports one, it will appear in `LLM_USAGE` with no code change. | 135 | 2026-09-27 | **The project's own test suite was spending real gateway tokens on every plain `pytest tests/` run; found, fixed and guarded. Tests only, plus docs; nothing to deploy.** **Found** while verifying the seeder: one test failed with a real connection timeout to `api.softwaresystems.app`. `tests/test_pipeline_review_diagnostic.py` holds four live tests (real classify and extract calls). The recorded token history never showed it because the test DB is reset per test. Two more leaks were found while fixing it: `tests/test_purge_request.py::test_the_owners_thumbnail_of_a_purge_requested_pdf_still_works_and_others_get_404` posted a real PDF with text (a deterministic real classify call), and every module that uploads a random-pixel JPEG can get junk text back from OCR and reach classify on SOME runs only (intermittent). The kill switch does not protect the suite: `tests/conftest.py`'s `_gateway_calls_switched_on` removes `LLM_CALLS_DISABLED` for every test on purpose. **Fixed:** (1) the four diagnostic tests need `RUN_LIVE_GATEWAY_TESTS=1` too (22 skipped by default, was 18); (2) `tests/conftest.py::no_real_network`, an autouse tripwire: any request through httpx's real transports to a non-loopback host is refused, RECORDED and asserted at teardown (the OpenAI SDK turns a transport exception into a connection error a test could swallow, the recorded attempt still fails it); loopback is left alone (the jaga-vision caption service on 127.0.0.1:8100 is free and unchanged); off only with `RUN_LIVE_GATEWAY_TESTS=1`; (3) `tests/conftest.py::_default_gateway_answers_are_canned`, an autouse default: the OpenAI SDK's `chat.completions.create` answers from a canned response (classify gets a 'memory' lane photo, so no extractor and no second call; any other tool gets no tool call), so the whole `app/llm.py::call()` path still runs; any test that patches `llm._client` or a node's `call` is unaffected; (4) the thumbnail test now cans its classify like `tests/test_upload_doc_type_hint.py`; (5) `tests/test_no_real_network.py` (4 tests) proves the tripwire, including that a real OpenAI client cannot leave the process and that loopback and Starlette's TestClient are not blocked. **Result:** 989 passed, 22 skipped; a run takes about 73 s instead of about 110 s because nothing calls out. **Size of the leak (an ESTIMATE, not a measurement):** four live tests at roughly 4 to 5k tokens each (the measured cost of one invoice through classify and extract, DECISIONS #129) is about 15 to 20k tokens per full run, plus the intermittent and thumbnail calls. The dashboard read 5,437,330 at about 15:45 WIB on 2026-09-26 and 5,736,702 at about 01:30 WIB on 2026-09-27 (+299,372) while the production box recorded no gateway calls (0 `LLM_USAGE` lines, no new trace rows): that is about 15 to 20 full suite runs by the advisor and peer sessions at the estimated cost. This is the leading explanation for the gap in #129 (+568,999 against about 22,000 recorded) and fits it better than a unit-conversion theory; it is UNPROVEN, because there is no gateway-side log and the test DB kept no record. It cannot be measured after the fact. **Commands:** plain `pytest tests/` (expect 22 skipped); to certify without any possibility of spend on an older checkout, `pytest tests/ --ignore=tests/test_pipeline_review_diagnostic.py`. NEVER `RUN_LIVE_GATEWAY_TESTS=1` without the user's explicit go. | A paid gateway that a test run can reach will be reached: the gate has to be a positive opt-in, and the guard has to sit at the network hop, because the switch, the key and each test's own mocks were all bypassed in turn. | The canned default means a test that silently depended on real model output would now see canned output instead (none is known: the only tests that asserted on real output were the gated live ones); the tripwire cannot see a client that does not use httpx (none exists in this codebase); the four live tests and `test_gateway_live.py` are no longer run by anyone unless they opt in. | final |

Added: `verify_seed_fixtures.photo_date_problems()` and invariant `every_seeded_photo_has_an_exif_picture_date` (the file's EXIF = the row's occurred_on = the `occurred_on` in the list the card is drawn from); `generate_seed_files.Gen.photo()` never emits a picture without a date (a missing one is stamped by `story_photo_date(cluster, caption)`, a pure function: a festival caption lands in that festival's window, a year-end caption in mid-December, otherwise a fixed day picked by crc32; never the random stream) and `main()` reads every rendered JPEG back and asserts its EXIF date equals `occurred_on`.
